Study note regarding the visit of Sub-Committee· VI of the Department Related Parliamentary 
Standing Committee on Industry 
1. The Sub-Committee VI of the Committee on Industry was constituted to study the 
problems of industrialisation in backward areas and capital goods industry with particular 
reference to the conditions of women participating and employed in this sector. The Sub­
Committee consisted of the following five members :-
1. Smt. Pratibha Devisingh Patil 
2. Smt. Suseela Gopalan 
3. Smt. Kesharbai Sonajirao Kshirsagar 
4. Smt. Sumitra Mahajan 
5. Smt. Chandrika Abhinandan Jain 
Smt. Pratibha Devisingh Patil was the Convenor of the Sub-Committee. 
The Sub-Committee visited Madhya Pradesh and Maharashtra from the 9th to 13th 
October, 1995 and held discussions with the ~presentatives of Chamber of Commerce and 
Industries, officers of the State Industry and other departments, .district authorities of 
Indore, Bombay and Pune and other women workers organisations~~ The salient features of 
the study are as under:-
Meeti11g with District Magistrate and Representatives of State Industry Department, lndor4 ._,, .. 
On the 9th October, 1995 the Sub-Committee heard the views Qf the officers of the 
State Industry Department and district authorities. The Sub-Cominittee was given to 
understand that the Industrial Policy and Action Plan, 1994 of the .Qpvernment of Madhya' 
Pradesh aims at accelerating and strengthening more capital investment, ensuring balanced 
regional development and providing additional employment opportunities for 
industrialisation in the State. This industrial policy also takes into account the liberalised 
economy at the national level as also changes at the international level. For this purpose the 
Government of Madhya Pradesh is committed to increase · the budgetary allocation for 
industry at present around 4% to 7% over next two years. The officials clarified before the 
Committee that the Government of Madhya Pradesh was keen for the industrial 
development of the State and a number of steps such as new opportunities for Small Scale 
Industries (SSI) and rural industries, greater· utilization of manpower, encouragement to 
women entrepreneurs and the entrepreneurs belonging to the Scheduled Castes, Scheduled 
Tribes and other Backward Classes and No'n-residential Indians, private sectors participation 
in ·infrastructure development, simplification of administrative procedure, promotion of 
cooperative sector for industrial development had been taken. 
231 
I • 
.. 

232 
Commenting on the specific efforts taken to promote SSI, the State Government 
officials apprised that the following special efforts had been made to promote the Small 
Scale Industries in the State:-
(i) Allotment of land at lower rate; 
(ii) help in procuring loan; 
(iii) capital subsidy of 15% (This subsidy has been abolished for medium and large 
scale industries); 
(iv) interest subsidy to the tune of Rs. 2500 for three years, -subsidy is paid directly to 
the institution; 
(v) sales and Commercial tax exemption in various forms; and 
(vi) development of infrastructure (23 Industrial Growth Centres have been 
developed). 
Apart .from this the "ABHIBHA YAK" (Guardian) Yojana has also been launched 
where a few small scale units are given to one Government, officer who helps the 
entrepreneurs in· getting their various administrative matters solved. For improving the 
position of finance to SSI, the State Government is working out a system for joint appraisal 
of a project by financial institutions and banks for the fixed and the working capital. The 
Madhya Pradesh Finance Corporation which generally provides term loan is also starting to 
provide working capital for small scale industries. 85% of the capital subsidy of 15% has also 
been given to small scale industries which have not gone into production. On the specific 
query of sickness in small scale industries, the State Government officials stated that 
according to 1991 census 40% of the small scale industries were sick due to various reasons 
but most of them are sick due to the lack of finance. The Sub-Committee was informed that 
though Single Window Scheme was not in operation in the strict sense but all the formalities 
for establishing an industrial unit was completed within three months and if there was any 
problems, a Committee headed by Collector existed in ea$!h district to help in the matter. 
Even in the industrial Growth Centres the entrepreneurs do not have; to run to respective 
district authorities to get appropriate sanctions. For this purpose representatives of various 
departments are available at one place at the Growth Centre itself to help the entrepreneurs. 
Since one of the main objects of the Sub-Committee was to study the conditions ;;.,;< 
women pafticipating and employed in various industries in the State it was deposed before 
the Committee that as envisaged in the Industrial Policy and Action Plan 1994, women 
entrepreneurship in the State were being encour1,1ged by giving priority in land allotment ::~iO 
additional tax concession for a further period of one year to the small scale industries set up 
by women entrepreneurs. It was also informed that there were 6.5 lakhs bidi ~::mployees in 
the State out of which 80% were women in the districts of Sagar, Damoh, Jabalpur and 
Satna. These bidi workers are getting Rs. 22.50 for per thousand bidi. On enquiring, the 
officials of the Labour Department informed that· these employees, were on contract basis 
and were getting less salary. There were also cases of delay payment, etc. It was also 
informed that cooperative sector in bidi which had been started in Jabalpur was not in good 
condition. On housing facilities for bidi workers, it was deposed that houses were being 
provided to bidi workers with the participation of the Central Government but such houses 
are very less in number. The bidi workers are also provided maternity benefit i.e., three 
months leave-one and a h,alf months before and one and a half months after the delivery. 
Creches are also ma<fe available to them. Where 30 women are employed, one creche is 
provided for them. fn 'other small scale industries, working hours, remuneration and 
minimum wages, welfare activities and safety measures for women provided .under various 

233 
laws are regulated byadministration. For women workers there are separate rooms and 
toilets in the factory. In certain industrial units subsidised transport facility is also provided. 
The performance of the State under the Prime Minister Rozgar Y ojana (PMR Y) is very 
encouraging and this year there is 110% achievement by the State in the field. However due 
to lack of reservation (although preference to women is envisaged in PMRY) for women 
nothing remarkable has been done for women. It was also informed that minimum wage Act 
was being adopted in all units. 
Visit to Growth Centre, Pithampur 
The Sub-Committee also visited a Growth Centre at Pithampur where it met the women 
workers in a diamond cutting industry separately. The Committee was told by the 
management that most of the women belonged to the needy class and only 12% of the 
workers are women because taking women in employment was highly expensive in Madhya 
Pradesh and despite the encouragement schemes like subsidised transport the response from 
women was very poor. The Committee was alsq told that women workers were generally less 
efficient as compared to their male counterpart though this was denied by women employees 
interviewed. There were total 62 workers with a monthly income of Rs. 16001-. One creche 
was there where only one Aya was provided to look after their children. Some medical 
facility was provided to women as are given to their male colleagues. They were getting a 
sum of Rs. 100/- as housing allowance. 
The Sub-Committee also visited a pharmaceutical industry and found that most of the 
workers were engaged in packing work and were being provided the necessary benefits as 
per the rules. Out of the total of 250 women workers 40 were engaged in packing and 
sorting work. Transport was provided to women worker and they were getting between 
Rs. 1000/- and Rs. 1200/- as wages and Rs. 1SO/- as bonus. No House Rent Allowance 
(HRA) was given to them. Three months maternity leave and during that period a sum of 
Rs. 3000/- were paid to them. No medical facility was given but during illness they got leave 
for which they were not paid. 
Visit to Readymade Garment Industry, Indore 
The Sub-Committee visited a readymade garment industry and had discussion with the 
women employees. The Sub-Committee was satisfied with the working conditions and 
facilities given to women employees in the industry. 
Visit to Bidi Making Factory, Indore 
The Sub-Committee came to know while discussing with the bidi workers that their 
main problem was of housing and even after serving for twenty-five to thirty years this 
facility was not given to them. Apart from this these pidi workers w~re getting only 
Rs. 22.50 per thousand bidi which was quite low. Women bz'di workers also informed the 
Committee that because of a dispute between the two partners, they had not been getting 
their wages for the last three months. 
Visit to ~mall Industries Service Institute (SIS/), Indore 
The Sub-Committee was informed that much emphasis had been given by the State 
Government on development of women entrepreneurship and for this purpose various 
entreprenurship in self employment assignment developmental programmes, especially 
product-cum-process oriented programme had been launched. The Sub-Committee a-lso came 
to know that SISI, Indore was the first institute in the nation which had a women cell for the 
upliftment of women. While deliberating with the trainees the Sub-Committee was informed 
that training in all walks of entrepreneurship, including procuring finance, marketing and 

publicity was imparted in the training programmes but the trainees were actually facing 
problem in obtaining loan and finding market for their products. 
The Sub-Committee, thereafter, held discussions with women entrepreneurs who 
informed that they were· facing difficulty due to:-
(i) Non-implementation of announced policy; 
(ii) lack of infrastructure; 
(iii) lack of power and its high rate; 
(iv) marketing of the product; and 
(v) recently announced provident fund scheme for workers. 
The Sub-Committee was given to undetstand that banks were reluctant to provide 
working capital even if the project had been held viable by·-DIC because they did not have 
confidence in the women; and male member would get benefit in the name of women. 
Banks were asking for collateral security and in few cases they. were also reluctant to 
provide loan for small projects of five to ten lakhs. 
Although PMRY envisaged that preference might be given to women but no 
percentage had been fixed undei\ the scheme for women due to which not much headway 
had been made in the field. 
MeetiRg with Workers, Bombay 
The Sub-Committee met women workers engaged in rubber, textile, food processing, 
pharmaceutical, tobacco and diamond and gold jewellery industries and during the 
discussion it was informed that barring one or two cases medical facilities including routine 
check-ups were provided in every establishment and women were also given maternity 
benefits. There were creches almost in every establishment. The Sub-Committee also came 
to know while holding discussion with women workers of a computer component 
manufacturing industry that the minimum wages have not been fixed for employees of 
service industries. 
The Sub-Committee observed that the conditions of women employees working in 
small scale units was not on a par with others because most of these- women were 
employed on contract basis. They were getting only Rs. 30/- per day with no other 
facilities. 
Meeting with Women Entrepreneurs and Representatives of Maharashtra Chamber of 
Commerce (Women Wing) 
, The Sub-Committee was informed that 20% of industrial flats had been reserved for 
women entrepreneurs by MIDC and this reservation had been fully utilised by women 
entrepreneurs. The main problem which the Sub-Committee was apprised of was the 
registeration of home-made products manufactured by women. Due to the lack of 
registration; women entrepreneures were finding it difficult to market their home-made 
products. Some women entrepreneures were also not getting perMission to manufacture 
home-made products in residential areas which according to them did not harm the place 
or disturb its peace. Some women also informed that though for their welfare many

implemented and for their implementation it took very long time. The Sub-Committee was 
informed that for PMRY banks were reluctant to· provide fund and even the Mahila 
Udyam Nidhi Scheme for women entrepreneures was also not running effectively.

submitted before the Sub-Committee that the excise rules may be revised to make them 

235 
simple and rational and banks/financial institutions may be directed to help SSI in order to

The Committee was informed that minimum wage had been fixed but most of the 
women were drawing more than minimum wage. In the unskilled sector women workers

Meeting with Representatives of Industry and Labour Department of the Government of 
Maharashtra 
The Committee was told that Maharashtra was the leading State in establishing· various 
kinds of industries in the country since 15% of the nation's factories wer.e located in the 
State and nearly one fourth of India's industria·! output was produced in Maharashtra. The 
Committee was given to understand that Maharashtra's new industrial policy announced in 
April, 1993 aimed at further sharpening of competitive edge of the State and strengthening 
its premier status. 
The most outstanding features of the policy are:-
(i) The revision of the industrial location policy for Bombay Metropolitan Region 
permitting non-polluting, high-tech industries within the rationalised municipal 
zones of Greater Bombay; 
(ii) non-requirement of the Collector's permission for purchase of land for industrial 
use and its NA conversion in certain areas; 
(iii) export-oriented agro processing projects permitted to hold land in excess of 
ceiling; and 
(iv) liberal benefits under the Package Scheme of Incentives and special benefits for 
100% Export-Oriented Unites (EOU), regardless of their location. 
Besides this the new Industrial Policy also provided for private participation in power 
projects and setting up of new industrial estate and infrastructure. There are thirteen 
corporations and Directorate of Industries which help in implementation of the State's 
Industrial Policy. SICOM a Government of Maharashtra undertaking provides versatile 
package of services for promotion of medium and large scale units. It also implements the 
State Government's Package Scheme of Incentive and works as nodal agency in Maharashtra 
extending all the assistance to NRI entrepreneures to set up Industrial units. The Sub­
Committee was apprised that the Udyog Mitra offered general guidance to entrepreneures 
seeking to set up industries, in respect of various rules and regulations, policy matters, 
approvals and clearances which had to be necessarily complied with at several stages during 
the progress of a project and also provided assistance for submission of formal requests to 
different organisations and in following up such requests for appropriate sanctions. 
On specific query on reluctance of banks to give working capital to the projects cleared 
by DIC or to the units which are sick, the Sub-Committee was apprised that the main 
reasons for reluctance of banks to provide working capital was due to the fact that many 
times the amount of loan required as working capital was very high since it was assessed by 
private organisation. In order to help industries financially, MSFC had also started granting 
financial assistance by way of seed capital besides giving term loan. 
The Sub-Committee was informed that there were many women in the electronic section 
and MEL TRON was giving training to women and helping them to get jobs on regular basis. 
The Sub-Committee wa~ told that DIC (District Industries Centre) had also started 
MCEDP (Maharashtra Centre for Entrepreneur Development Programme) which was

reservation for women, the Sub-Committee was infonned that reservation for women 
member was provided in the managing committee. which looked after day to day 
management in the following. manner:-
( a) One seat on the Committee consisting of not more than· 9 members' Committee;

Committee members; 
(c) Three seats on the Committee consisting of 20 or more Committee members. 
As per the above provision, there were 2 or 3 women Directors on the Commi-.e of 
most of the sugar factories depending upon the total strength of Directors and there" were 
5 sugar factories which are having women as Chairpersons. 
Commenting on women labourers, the Commissioner told that labourers were required 
for harvesting and transporting sugarcane from field to factorie~ and these were temporary 
workers ~ho were not employed directly by factories. 
At present there are about 2 lakhs female workers. Since- they are not employed by 
factories they do not get salary and other benefits. However, besides reward at the time of 
closure the following facilities are given:-
(1) Medical facilities at factory site; 
(2) Anganwadi or. primary school at factory site; 
(3) Maternity benefits; 
(4) Family Planning operations at the cost of the factory; 
(5) Arrangement for bringing them from their place to the factory site and sending 
them back to · their places; 
(6) Some factories give dresses at the time to closure; 
(7) Facilities for water, electricity, etc. at factory site; and 
(8) Some factories hcrve given insurance facilities to tl* labourers. 
Meeting with District CoHector and Representative of State Industry and Education 
Department , 
The Sub-Committee was informed that Pune was one of the industrially developed 
districts in Maharashtra. The Sub-Committee was .also given to. understand that there were 
275 large and medium industries in Pune district with the total investment of Rs. 9930 
crores. There were also 21391 permanent SSI registered units in the district and for the 
benefit of entrepreneurs simple and modified process had been adopted in the office. For 
coordination and problems solving the meetia..g of Zila Udyog Mitra Committee was 
convened once in every month:-under the Chairmanship of the District Collector. On the 
position of women entrepreneurs the Sub-'Committee was told that following special 
measures in respect of infrastructural and credit facilities had been taken:-
(i) MIDC has resolved to keep !b% reservation of plots, sheds for women 
entrepreneurs up to one year. 
(li) Municipal Corporation Cquncil gives preference for allotment of constructed sheds 
to women beneficiaries for self employment. 
(iii) Preference is given in allotment of Plot and shed by cooperative industrial estate. 
(iv) Priority is given in telephone connection for women entrepreneurs.

(v) Various special schemes have been launched by MSFC, BOI, KVIB for women 
entrepreneurs. 
Apart from this, Mahila Udyog Nidhi an exclusive xheme of providing equity support 
to wamen entrepreneurs la11nched by SIDBI is also under Gperation and preference to 
women entrepreneurs is also given by PMRY, Seed Money Scheme and DIC Margin money 
Scheme. The Committee came to know that in marketing Mahila Vikas Mahamandal 1lnd 
Maharashtra SSI Development Corporation was helping women entrepreneurs. The Sub­
Committee felt that the various scheme launched shou''td not be on papers only and must be 
put into practice if women entrepreneurs were to be given benefits in real terms. 
On the status of literacy amongst women in Maharashtra the Sub-Committee was 
informed that the female literacy rate in the State is 52.32% which is slightly above the 
national female literacy rate of 39.29%. The Sub-Committee was further apprised that total 
literacy campaign was being implemented for eradication of illiteracy in the State and right 
now thi~ campaign was being implemented in 13 districts. The Sub-Committee was happy to 
know th\tt the response of women in this total literacy campaigns was more encouraging than 
men. The Sub-Committee expressed its satisfaction over the percentage of women literacy in 
the State and hoped that with the help of total literacy campaigns the literacy of women 
would further increase. 
Visit to Handmade Paper Industry, Pune 
The Sub-Committee had a round of various stages of manufacturing of handmade paper 
and expressed its opinion that more such environment friendly units might be set up. 
Visit to Telco Grihini Udyog, Pune 
The Sub-Committee visited· the Telco Grihini Udyog where it was informed that Telco 
Grihini movement was started so that housewives were provided with opportunity to work in 
spare time. The Sub-Committee also came to know that Telco Grihini Udyog had'come a 
long way from making ordinary articles to the wide range of products which included spices, 
condiments and engineering component required for Telco's assembly lines. 
The Sub-Committee expressed its satisfaction over the functioning of Telco Grihini 
Udyog. 
On the basis of the discussion held by the Committee with different Associations, 
Workers Union, representatives of Stat~ Governments of Maharashtra and Madhya Pradesh, 
the Committee arrived at certain conclusions. Some of the important observations/ 
recommendations of the Committee are as under : 
1.0 Considering the problems of bidi workers, the Sub-Committee recommends that the 
housing problem of the bidi work~rs may be looked into sympathetically an,d more houses 
may be provided to them as early as possible. Their wages may be increased and disputes 
involving their demands may be settled. The working condition of the bidi workers in the 
factory are far from satisfactory and in most of the cases unhygenic. The Committee 
recommends that their working conditions need to be considerly improved by the 
Government. 
1.1 On the basis of the discussion held with small entrepreneurs, the Committee 
recommends that it should be made compulsory that whatever products women entrepreneurs 
mak'e in the small units SO'Ine percentage of it must be purchased by the ·Government 
departments. 
1.2 Job sheds system should be introduced where women will come and work on difficult 
projects under one roof and get proper remuneration. The central worksheds for women 

r ... 
240 
sbctuld be opened in eaeb district which are financed by the State Government itself or set up 
with the help of KVIC. There should be a Joint Committee of social workers and Government 
representatives to supervise and coordinate the marketing of the product produced in these 
worksbeds. 
1.3 The Committee recommends that dler.e should be women cooperative banks in each 
district and 50% matching contribution be allocated by the Government for &ranting the loan 
to ladies who are small entrepreneurs. 
1.4 The Sub-Committee also recommends that with a view to ensuring effective financial 
arrangement a scheme may be evolved for joint appraisal of projects by financial institution 
and banks for the requirement of the small scale units for the fixed and the working capital as 
early as possible. For revival of sick units in Madhya Pradesh which at present are suffering 
from the paucity of finance, the Sub-Committee recommends that Madhya Pradesh Finance 
Corporation must ini~iate for providing. finance after getting the viability examined by DIC. 
· 1.5 The Sub-Committee recommends that some percentage of Prime Minister Rozgar 
Yojana may be fixed for women and their project must be sanctioned after due care so that 
the provision of reservation is not exploited so as to defeat the main objectives of the Yojana. 
1.6 With regard to the condition of working women the Sub-Committee recommends that 
as has been done in the case of other industries, minimum wages for, small scale industries 
may also be fixed depending on the economic condition and viability of the SSI unit. The Sub­
Committee further recommends that the question of enhancing the earning and improving the 
working conditions of the women workers may be dealt with sympathetically. 
; 
1.7 On tbe,basis of the discussion with Maharashtra Chamber of Commerce and Industry 
(Ladies win&), the Sub-Committee is of the view that State Government must. find ways to 
help the women eQgaged . in manufacturing of home made product to get their product 
marketed. They should be provided business licences at their residential permises~ The Sub· 
Committee feels that for the first five years concession be given in electric tarrif which will 
definitely help the small entrepreneurs. Some concession should also be given to them with 
regard to sale tax, income tax, etc. 
1.8 The Committee feels that the objectives of MA VIM in Maharashtra have not come 
upto the mark and its working should be re-organised. Voluntary o~·ganisations should be 
encouraged and helped by MA VIM like institutions in each State. Voluntary organisations 
should al.so be encouraged to give employment to needy women. The Committee also endorses 
the view of . reservation of women in all vocational training course to enhance their 
participation in industrial sector in the country. 
1.9 On the basis of the discussion held with the representatives of the Khadi and Village 
Industry, Bo~nbay, the Sub-Conunittee recommends that in order to increase the earning of 
charkha weavers, modern charkhas on subsidised rates may be provided to the weavers. The 
Sub-Committee further recommends that to boost the export of Khadi and Village Industries 
products, the quality of products have to be improved in order to compete. For the purpose of 
giving impetus to export, representation in the Export llfomotion Council may be given to 
KVIC. The Sub-Committee also recommends that the National Renewal Fund may be utilised 
for the purpose of training the person and their re-deployment. The Sub-Committee expresses 
its displeasure over the red-tapism in KVIC and hopes that it will devE!Iop into a public office 
sheding its bureaucratic attitude. 
2. The Sub-Committee feels that Khadi and Village Industry has done pride to the nation 
and its activities should not be neglected. For this purpose the Sub-Committee 

241 
recommends KVIC may be given an independefft financial status and should not be left at the 
mercy of RBI or other banks. The Committee recommends that there should be a specific cell 
in KVIC for promoting women entrepreneurs. For this purpose, the Committee feels, that 
KVIC should be provided some budgetary support.' 
2.1 On the basis of the discussion held with representatives of the National Informatics 
Centre, Pune, the Committee suggests that the centre can feed data about women 
agriculturists, their minimum wages fixed in each State and other facilities provided to them. 
The Sub-Committee expresses its satisfaction over the functioning of NIC in the region. 
2.2 On the basis of the discussion held with social workers, the Committee has learnt that 
the daily wages workers are getting very little amount. Most of them are unrecognised 
workers and are being exploited by their employer. The Committee strongly feels that their 
condition should be improved by paying them minimum wages declared by the Government 
and special legislation may be brought forward to help them. 
2.3 The Committee recommends that food processing units run by women entrepreneurs 
should be provided special assistance by the Food Processing Ministry • 
... 
2.4 The Sub-Committee feels that some institutional support for increasing their 
knowledge should be provided to women entrepreneurs who are engaged in making papad, 
pickles and mid-day meal work. They may also be provided some financial, technical and 
marketing support. 
2.5 On the basis of the discussion held with Sugar Commissioner, Pune the Sub­
Committee felt that mobile creches should be provided to women workers during tbe working 
season. Literacy camp should also be started in the factory premises. The Sub-Committee 
recommends that the minimum wages of the women workers should be fixed strictly and the 
provisions relating to them should be implemented accordingly. 
2.6 The Sub-Committee held the discussion with district officials and State industry 
department. The Committee was informed that various schemes are operating for upliftment 
of women entrepreneurs. The Sub-Committee is of the view that the various schemes launched 
, by the State Governments should not be on the paper only and these must be put into 
practice, if women entrepreneurs are to be provided benefits in real terms. 
The Sub-Committee was also informed that women's work in the construction industry is 
not recognised. Most women fall in the category of unskilled workers and there is no effort to 
promote them as skilled worker and improve their earning capacity. 
The Sub-Committee suggests for removing disparity in wages between men and women 
employed in the industry as well as ensure minimum wages to women. The Sub-Committee 
further suggests for the compulsory registration of women workers, provision of creches,; 
housing facilities, extension of ESI scheme and Provident Fund benefit, mobile medical service 
to non-ESI category of workers. The Sub-Committee feels that the facilities mentioned above 
should have t>rovided to the women engaged in construction work.
